Critics highlighted the negative effects it would have on the [separation of powers](74576.md#Separation_of_Powers),[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] the office of the [Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_Israel),[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] the economy,[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] public health,[^cite_note-Izak-2023-269][^cite_note-Barnea-2023-270] women and minorities,[^cite_note-Barnea-2023-270][^cite_note-271] workers' rights,[^cite_note-272] scientific research,[^cite_note-Barnea-2023-270][^cite_note-273] the strength of Israel's democracy[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] and its foreign relations.[^cite_note-276] After [public protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Israeli_judicial_reform_protests), joined by [military reservists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_reserve_force), Minister of Defense [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) spoke against the reform on 25 March, calling for a halt "for the sake of Israel's security".[^cite_note-277] Netanyahu announced his intention to remove Gallant, sparking further protests and leading to Netanyahu agreeing to delay the legislation for a month.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279]

In February 2023, the government approved the legalization of nine [settler outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost) in the occupied West Bank.[^cite_note-280] Finance Minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) took charge of most of the [Civil Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Civil_Administration), obtaining broad authority over civilian issues in the West Bank. Israeli peace groups condemned the move as *de jure* [annexation of the occupied territories](60995835.md).[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282][^cite_note-NYT-283] In March, Netanyahu's government repealed a 2005 law whereby four Israeli settlements, [Homesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homesh), [Sa-Nur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sa-Nur), [Ganim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganim) and [Kadim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadim), were dismantled as part of the [Israeli disengagement from Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza).[^cite_note-284] In June, Netanyahu's coalition shortened the procedure of approving settlement construction, and gave Smotrich authority to approve one of the stages, changing the system operating for the previous 27 years.[^cite_note-285] In its first six months, construction of 13,000 housing units in settlements, almost triple the amount advanced in the whole of 2022.[^cite_note-286]



Netanyahu with U.S. president [Joe Biden](145422.md) in New York City, 20 September 2023

Israel refused to [send lethal weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) to [Ukraine](31750.md). In June 2023, Netanyahu said Israel is concerned "with the possibility that systems that we would give to Ukraine would fall into Iranian hands and could be reverse engineered, and we would find ourselves facing Israeli systems used against Israel."[^cite_note-287]

On 7 October 2023, after Palestinian militants from Gaza launched [a major surprise attack](74998317.md), Netanyahu announced that Israel would enter a war against Hamas.[^cite_note-288] He threatened to "turn all the places where Hamas is organized and hiding into cities of ruins", called [Gaza](55387.md) "the city of evil", and urged its residents to "leave now".[^cite_note-289] He proposed opposition parties Yesh Atid and National Unity enter [an emergency unity government](75030844.md),[^cite_note-290] after Leader of the Opposition Lapid urged Netanyahu put "aside our differences and form an emergency, narrow, professional government."[^cite_note-291]

&#x20;Netanyahu was criticized for presiding over Israel's biggest intelligence failure in 50 years, and has [faced protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war_protests_in_Israel#%22Bring_Them_Home_Now%22_protests) calling for his removal. The war led to increased opposition to Netanyahu due to failure to anticipate the Hamas-led attack, with increased calls for Netanyahu's resignation.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294] A 2023 poll showed that 56% of Israelis believed Netanyahu must resign after the war, with 86% holding the country's leadership responsible for the security failings that led to the attack.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298] On 28 October, Netanyahu was accused of using "dangerous rhetoric" when comparing Hamas to [Amalek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amalek), stating: "You must remember what Amalek has done to you, says our Holy Bible. And we do remember."[^cite_note-299] On 29 October, Netanyahu blamed Israel's security chiefs for Hamas's attack in a post on X; this was deleted following criticism.[^cite_note-300] An investigation carried out by [Shin Bet](311752.md) acknowledged the agency's failures, but found that Shin Bet warned the prime minister that Hamas was not deterred, and objected to the divide-and-rule policy vis-a-vis Hamas and the Palestinian Authority.[^cite_note-shabak-301]



Netanyahu addresses a joint session of Congress in Washington, D.C. with House Speaker [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson) and Senator [Ben Cardin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Cardin), 24 July 2024.

In November, he rejected calls for a ceasefire and warned that Israel will "stand firm against the world if necessary." He said the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) would remain in Gaza "as long as necessary" and Israel would prevent the Palestinian Authority from returning to Gaza.[^cite_note-302] Netanyahu called allegations that Israel is breaking international law "hogwash" and described Palestinian [civilian casualties](75130785.md) as "collateral damage".[^cite_note-303] In December 2023, he faced criticism during a meeting with released [Israeli hostages](75025451.md). One hostage accused him of putting politics "above the return of the kidnapped."[^cite_note-304] Netanyahu said Israel should support the "voluntary migration" of Palestinians from Gaza.[^cite_note-305]

Israel under Netanyahu has been accused of committing genocide in Gaza in the [*South Africa v. Israel*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel_\(Genocide_Convention\)) case before the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) in December 2023.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307] In May 2024, [Karim Khan](11606957.md), the [prosecutor of the International Criminal Court](33903993.md), announced his intention to apply for an [arrest warrant](146746.md) for Netanyahu on counts of alleged [war crimes](48626.md) and [crimes against humanity](61099.md).[^cite_note-ICCAW-308][^cite_note-ICCAW_CNN-309]

In July 2024, Netanyahu addressed another [joint session of the United States Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_session_of_the_United_States_Congress), amidst widespread protest, to solicit support for the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-310] He called protesters "[useful idiots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Useful_idiot)" and pledged a "total victory" in Gaza.[^cite_note-311] He met with 2024 Republican nominee for president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) at his [Mar-a-Lago](1452754.md) residence where he criticized Democratic nominee [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) for vowing that she "will not be silent" about atrocities in Gaza.[^cite_note-312]



Netanyahu and U.S. Secretary of Defense [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) at the Pentagon on 5 February 2025&#x20;

In October 2024, a [drone attack](38212231.md) believed to have originated from Lebanon [was made on Netanyahu's residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_drone_attack_on_Benjamin_Netanyahu's_residence) in [Caesarea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesarea_\(modern_town\)). Netanyahu was not in the residence, and no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-313] Netanyahu accused [Hezbollah](13919.md) of trying to assassinate him.[^cite_note-314]

On 5 November 2024, Netanyahu fired defense minister Gallant, who had advocated for a short-term diplomatic deal. The firing triggered protests throughout Israel.[^cite_note-315] On 21 November, the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) issued arrest warrants for Netanyahu, Gallant and Hamas military commander [Mohammed Deif](92534.md) for [alleged war crimes](75113830.md) committed during the Gaza war.[^cite_note-316] Netanyahu described the ruling as "absurd and false lies" and said the decision is "antisemitic".[^cite_note-317]

On the night of 18 March 2025, Israel launched a [surprise attack on the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Israeli_attacks_on_the_Gaza_Strip), effectively ending the [January 2025 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire).[^cite_note-318] Netanyahu was scheduled on 18 March to testify in [his corruption trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Benjamin_Netanyahu), but as a result of the attacks, the proceedings were postponed.[^cite_note-319] In March 2025, Netanyahu fired [Ronen Bar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronen_Bar), chief of [Shin Bet](311752.md), citing a loss of confidence.[^cite_note-320] Bar accused the government of firing him for investigating [Qatar](8238258.md)'s involvement and influence in the Prime Minister's Office, in a case known as the [Qatari connection affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatari_connection_affair).[^cite_note-321]



Netanyahu with President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [Leo Terrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Terrell), the head of the Trump administration's [Task Force to Combat Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Task_Force_to_Combat_Anti-Semitism), 7 April 2025

In May, Netanyahu stated that the destruction of homes in Gaza would lead to the [forced emigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_evacuations) of Palestinians.[^cite_note-322]

On 13 June, Netanyahu authorized airstrikes against Iran, marking the beginning of the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War).[^cite_note-unprecedented-323] Within a week, Netanyahu publicly commented on the effects of the war: "Each of us bears a personal cost, and my family has not been exempt", as he cited that it was "the second time that my son Avner has cancelled a wedding due to missile threats."[^cite_note-324] In July, Netanyahu was criticized for deciding to increase Gaza aid distribution by some in his cabinet, who rejected the U.S. ceasefire proposal.[^cite_note-325]

In August 2025, Netanyahu said in an interview that he was on a "historic and spiritual mission" and "very" attached to the vision of [Greater Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Israel),[^cite_note-326] which includes the Palestinian territories and other Arab states.[^cite_note-327] On 21 September, Netanyahu rejected the existence of a Palestinian state west of the [Jordan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_River), saying, "For years, I have prevented the establishment of this terrorist state facing tremendous pressures at home and abroad."[^cite_note-328] In Netanyahu's address to the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md), he opposed international recognition of a Palestinian state. As Netanyahu commenced his speech, dozens of individuals exited the hall in protest.[^cite_note-329]



Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) is welcomed by Netanyahu on his arrival in Israel, 25 February 2026

On 7 November, the Chief Public Prosecutor's Office in [Turkey](11125639.md) issued international arrest warrants for Netanyahu and 36 other Israeli officials, accusing them of genocide and crimes against humanity in Gaza.[^cite_note-330] The charges were based on Israel's attacks on civilians, hospitals, infrastructure and the [Global Sumud Flotilla](80704925.md), which was intercepted by the Israeli navy while delivering aid to Gaza.[^cite_note-331]

Under Netanyahu's premiership, Israel became the first country to recognize [Somaliland](51425.md), a breakaway region of [Somalia](27358.md), as an independent state, in December 2025.[^cite_note-332]

In March 2026, after Israel dropped charges against Israeli soldiers accused of sexual assault at the [Sde Teiman detention camp](76887648.md), Netanyahu said "Israel must pursue its enemies – not its heroic fighters".[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335][^cite_note-336]

In February 2026, Israel and the United States launched a [major military offensive against Iran](82533582.md) with the stated goal of inducing [regime change](37258993.md).[^cite_note-337] According to multiple diplomatic sources, Netanyahu lobbied U.S. President Donald Trump to initiate the joint strikes.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339]

In April 2026, Netanyahu said "There is no [ceasefire](82888027.md) in Lebanon. We are [continuing to strike](82903314.md) Hezbollah with full force, and we will not stop until we restore your security."[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342]

In July 2026, speaking in an interview with [Channel 14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_14_\(Israel\)), Netanyahu said that the Gaza Strip was "no longer" a military threat to Israel, and that his decision to strike Iran had been "to save us from annihilation by nuclear bombs that were already in their hands", the latter statement drawing criticism from political rivals [Gadi Eisenkot](5249926.md) and Naftali Bennett, who disputed that Iran had ever possessed nuclear weapons.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] In the same interview, Netanyahu cited his ongoing corruption trial as evidence for the need for further [judicial reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_reform).[^cite_note-346]

On 8 September 2026, [Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz) published an exposé claiming that Netanyahu had received advanced warning from UAE President [Mohamed bin Zayed](2121945.md) of the 7 October attacks days before, but did not warn Israeli security chiefs.[^cite_note-347] In response, Netanyahu denied the reporting as an "absolute lie" and announced that he will file a lawsuit against Haaretz for allegedly committing [libel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libel) against him through this exposé.[^cite_note-348]

Netanyahu's tenures as prime minister have been marked by accusations of [authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism) and [democratic backsliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding), including efforts to overhaul the judicial system in 2023 that critics argued were intended to protect him from corruption charges.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-court2-350] His 2022 coalition government was described as the most right-wing in Israeli history.[^cite_note-351] He has supported stricter immigration controls and has called for Israel to assume long-term security responsibility over Gaza following the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-352]

Netanyahu has opposed the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords) and was described by peace envoy [Dennis Ross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Ross) as having "no real interest in pursuing peace."[^cite_note-353] In 2001, he was recorded stating he would interpret the Oslo Accords in a way that would prevent a return to the 1967 borders.[^cite_note-washingtonpost2-354] He has advocated for an "economic peace" approach focusing on economic cooperation rather than political resolution.[^cite_note-haaretz_11-21-082-355] For years, he backed Qatari payments to Gaza, stating in 2019 that supporting Hamas was part of a strategy to prevent Palestinian statehood.[^cite_note-Vox15Oct2-356] He publicly supported the [Trump peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Trump_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_plan) in 2020. Netanyahu also played a leading role in the [Abraham Accords](64883396.md), normalizing relations with the UAE, Bahrain, Sudan, and Morocco.[^cite_note-357]

Netanyahu has described Iran as the greatest threat to Israel's existence, comparing it to Nazi Germany, and has been a prominent opponent of the [Iran nuclear deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_nuclear_deal).[^cite_note-Haaretz160420082-358][^cite_note-359] In 2025, he authorized airstrikes against Iran's nuclear facilities, and in 2026, he launched joint strikes with the U.S. that assassinated the Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) and other officials, starting a major regional war. He has framed these campaigns as opportunities for regime change in Iran.[^cite_note-360] Netanyahu has written extensively on counter-terrorism, arguing that democracies must balance civil liberties with security measures while treating terrorism as a form of totalitarianism.[^cite_note-361]

A proponent of free-market policies, Netanyahu introduced significant reforms as finance minister, including privatization, tax reductions, and welfare-to-work programs.[^cite_note-Likud_Leaders_20152-362] Supporters credited these reforms with increasing foreign investment and economic growth, while critics argued that they contributed to greater economic inequality.[^cite_note-363] He supports equal rights for the [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) community and has spoken against racism and discrimination toward Ethiopian Israelis.[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365] In 2026, he supported legislation applying the death penalty to Palestinians convicted of terrorism.[^cite_note-366]

## Relations with foreign leaders



Netanyahu with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) at the [2018 Moscow Victory Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade). The two leaders have had a close relationship.

Serving as prime minister in three nonconsecutive periods since the 1990s,[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368] he developed close relationships with foreign leaders. Netanyahu has a close relationship with Hungarian prime minister [Viktor Orbán](750582.md), their having known each other for decades due to the privileged relationship between the Likud Party and the EPP, the [European People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_People's_Party). Orban particularly admired Netanyahu while he was working as finance minister, and received advice from him while Netanyahu was Finance Minister of Israel.[^cite_note-369]

Netanyahu has been noted for his close and friendly relationship with former-late Italian prime minister [Silvio Berlusconi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silvio_Berlusconi).[^cite_note-Haaretz20110614-370] Netanyahu has said of Berlusconi: "We are lucky that there is a leader such as yourself."[^cite_note-371] Netanyahu has described Berlusconi as "one of the greatest friends".[^cite_note-Haaretz20110614-370][^cite_note-telegraph.co.uk-372]

Netanyahu and Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) developed a close relationship and [ties between India and Israel](7199609.md) increased during their rule.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375]

Netanyahu had a warm relationship and "personal friendship" with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md).[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] In his 2022 book, Netanyahu wrote positively about Putin and describes him as "smart, sophisticated and focused on one goal – returning Russia to its historical greatness".[^cite_note-Netanyahu_Putin_Kuleba-378] Their relationship has been strained since the start of the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-379]



Netanyahu with [Viktor Orbán](750582.md) and other [V4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group) leaders at the V4-Israel summit in Budapest, Hungary on 19 July 2017



Netanyahu and Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) in New Delhi, India on 14 January 2018. The two leaders have had a close relationship.

[^cite_note-380]

In early 2018, the Polish parliament adopted a [new Polish law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amendment_to_the_Act_on_the_Institute_of_National_Remembrance) criminalizing suggestions that Poles were collectively complicit in [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust)-related or other war crimes that had been committed during [World War II](32927.md) by the [Axis powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_powers).[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] Later that year at the Munich Security Conference, Polish prime minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md) said "it is not going to be seen as criminal to say that there were Polish perpetrators, as there were Jewish perpetrators ... not only German perpetrators" implicated in the Jewish Holocaust.[^cite_note-383] Netanyahu called his Polish counterpart's comment "outrageous" for saying that Jews had been among the Holocaust's perpetrators.[^cite_note-384] The resulting crisis in [Israel–Poland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Poland_relations) was resolved in late June that year when the two prime ministers issued a joint communiqué endorsing research into the Jewish Holocaust and condemning the misnomer "[Polish concentration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%22Polish_death_camp%22_controversy)".[^cite_note-385]



Netanyahu with Argentine president [Javier Milei](56599840.md) in Jerusalem, June 2025

According to [Efraim Zuroff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efraim_Zuroff) of the [Simon Wiesenthal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Wiesenthal_Center), during the visit of [Ukrainian](31750.md) President [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) in Jerusalem, Netanyahu failed to publicly address [Ukraine's official policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_decommunization_laws) of rehabilitating local Nazi collaborators like [UPA](1045217.md) leader [Roman Shukhevych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Shukhevych), who had participated in the murder of Jews.[^cite_note-386]

Netanyahu had developed a close relationship with Brazilian president [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md) following Bolsonaro's 2018 election.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388] Netanyahu has also developed a good relationship with Argentinian president [Javier Milei](56599840.md), having called him a "great friend of the Jewish State", shortly after Milei started his presidential tenure.[^cite_note-389]

Since 2023, Netanyahu and Chinese president [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) have been engaged in diplomacy, arising due to strained ties between the US and Israel.[^cite_note-390] The diplomatic situation has been made complicated due to the [Gaza war](74998519.md), where China has remained neutral.[^cite_note-391]

Netanyahu and Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) have tense relations.[^cite_note-392] In March 2019, after being denounced by Turkey as a racist for saying that Israel was the nation-state of the Jewish people only, Netanyahu called Erdoğan a dictator and mocked him for imprisoning journalists in a tweet.[^cite_note-AP_Erdogan-393] In response, Erdoğan called Netanyahu as "the thief who heads Israel", referencing the ongoing corruption scandals against Netanyahu. In the same speech, Erdoğan further escalated the spat by addressing to Netanyahu directly, saying, "you are a tyrant. You are a tyrant who slaughters 7-year-old Palestinian kids",[^cite_note-AP_Erdogan-393] and further in April 2018, calling Israel "terror state" and Netanyahu "terrorist".[^cite_note-394] Netanyahu tweeted that "Erdoğan is among Hamas's biggest supporters and there is no doubt that he well understands terrorism and slaughter."[^cite_note-395] Netanyahu condemned the [2019 Turkish offensive into north-eastern Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Turkish_offensive_into_north-eastern_Syria) and warned against ethnic cleansing of [Kurds](3821855.md) by Turkey and its proxies.[^cite_note-Haaretz-396]

### US leaders

Netanyahu has close ties with the congressional leadership of the [U.S. Republican Party](32070.md) and with its 2012 presidential candidate, [Mitt Romney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney). He and Romney first became acquainted when both worked at the [Boston Consulting Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Consulting_Group) in the mid-1970s.[^cite_note-397]

During the [2011 G-20 Cannes summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_G-20_Cannes_summit), then-French president [Nicolas Sarkozy](557007.md) was overheard saying to then-U.S. president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama), "I cannot bear Netanyahu, he's a liar", and Obama reportedly responded, "You're fed up with him, but I have to deal with him every day."[^cite_note-YNet_Overheard-398][^cite_note-CBS_Fed_Up-399]



Netanyahu with [Joe Biden](145422.md) and [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md), 18 October 2023

In October 2014, author [Jeffrey Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Goldberg) related a conversation in which Goldberg said that a senior official of the Obama administration called Netanyahu a "chickenshit" after Netanyahu accused U.S. president Barack Obama of "acting contrary to American values". Goldberg went on to say that Netanyahu and his cabinet were largely to blame for the tensions between the Netanyahu and Obama governments.[^cite_note-crisis-176] Secretary of State [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) phoned Netanyahu to clarify that "such statements are disgraceful, unacceptable and damaging" and "do not reflect the position of the United States".[^cite_note-400] Netanyahu responded by saying "I'm being attacked because of my determination to defend Israel's interests. The safety of Israel is not important to those who attack me anonymously and personally."[^cite_note-401] Because of evident rifts between Netanyahu and members of the Obama administration, observers have characterized the relationship as having reached a crisis level by October 2014.[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-crisis-176] The relationship between Netanyahu and the Obama administration had become problematic enough that Goldberg reported that his conversations with Netanyahu and other Israeli officials indicated that Israel would wait until after the [2016 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election) before attempting to repair the relationship with the White House. According to [Alon Pinkas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alon_Pinkas), "Netanyahu's self-righteousness that this resolution is going to be changed or reversed by Trump is totally unfounded."[^cite_note-403]

On 23 December 2016, the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) passed [a resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2334) calling for an end to [Israeli settlements](15123.md).[^cite_note-404] In a departure from longstanding American policy, the U.S., under the Obama administration, abstained from the vote and did not exercise its [veto power](1900272.md). At the behest of the Netanyahu government, President-elect Trump attempted to intercede by publicly advocating for the resolution to be vetoed, as well as successfully persuading Egypt's [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md) to temporarily withdraw it from consideration.[^cite_note-405] The resolution was then "proposed again by [Malaysia](3607937.md), [New Zealand](4913064.md), [Senegal](27278.md) and [Venezuela](32374.md)" – and passed 14 to 0. Netanyahu's office alleged that "the Obama administration not only failed to protect Israel against this gang-up at the UN, it colluded with it behind the scenes", adding: "Israel looks forward to working with President-elect Trump and with all our friends in Congress, Republicans and Democrats alike, to negate the harmful effects of this absurd resolution."[^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408]



Netanyahu and [Donald Trump](4848272.md) during the signing of the [Abraham Accords](64883396.md) on 15 September 2020

Netanyahu and U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) have known each other for many years.[^cite_note-409] Netanyahu had been a friend of Donald Trump's father, [Fred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Trump), when Netanyahu lived in New York during the 1980s, serving as UN ambassador.[^cite_note-nymag.com-41] In 2013, Trump made a video endorsing Netanyahu during the [Israeli elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Israeli_legislative_election) saying, "vote for Benjamin – terrific guy, terrific leader, great for Israel".[^cite_note-410] In June 2019, Netanyahu officially renamed a [settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_Heights) in the disputed [Golan Heights](7515865.md) after Donald Trump.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] However, Trump aide [Jared Kushner](6191053.md) has claimed that in January 2020, Trump became frustrated with Netanyahu's rhetoric regarding annexation of the Jordan Valley, and considered endorsing his political opponent, [Benny Gantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Gantz).[^cite_note-413] Following Netanyahu's congratulations for [Joe Biden](145422.md) after the [2020 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_U.S._presidential_election), the relationship deteriorated, with Trump accusing him of disloyalty and stating Netanyahu had "made a terrible mistake".[^cite_note-414] Netanyahu and Trump repaired their relationship in July 2024 amid the lead-up to the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md),[^cite_note-415] in which Netanyahu endorsed Trump.[^cite_note-416] Netanyahu congratulated Trump's reelection to a [second presidential term](78298108.md) as "history's greatest comeback,"[^cite_note-417] and later asserted that Trump was "the greatest friend that Israel has ever had in the White House."[^cite_note-418]

U.S. president [Joe Biden](145422.md), a [Democrat](5043544.md), has been friendly with Netanyahu for many years. In November 2011[^cite_note-419] and in the [2012 U.S. vice presidential debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_election_debates,_2012),[^cite_note-420] Biden stated that the relationship has lasted for 39 years. In March 2010, Netanyahu remarked during a joint statement with Biden during his visit Israel that their friendship had started almost three decades prior.[^cite_note-421] During Spring 2024, the relationship between the two leaders had become strained over Israel's [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive).[^cite_note-422]

### Marriages and relationships



Netanyahu lighting [Hanukkah](7388.md) [candles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menorah_\(Hanukkah\)) on the first night in the [prime minister's office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Aghion) in [Jerusalem](16043.md) with his wife, [Sara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Netanyahu) and their sons, [Yair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yair_Netanyahu) and Avner, 1996

Netanyahu has been married three times and has been involved in multiple [extramarital affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affair). Netanyahu's first marriage was to Miriam Weizmann, whom he met in Israel. Weizmann lived near Yonatan Netanyahu's apartment in Jerusalem, where Netanyahu was based during his military service. By the time Netanyahu's service was finished, Weizmann had completed her own military service as well as a degree in chemistry from the [Hebrew University of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University_of_Jerusalem). In 1972, they both left to study in the United States, where she enrolled in [Brandeis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandeis_University), while Netanyahu studied at MIT. They married soon afterward. The couple had one daughter, Noa (born 29 April 1978).[^cite_note-Haaretz-2009-423][^cite_note-Miskin-2009-424]

In 1978, while Weizmann was pregnant, Netanyahu met a non-Jewish British student named Fleur Cates at the university library, and began an affair. His marriage ended in divorce soon after Miriam discovered the affair. In 1981, Netanyahu married Cates, and she [converted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conversion_to_Judaism) to [Judaism](15624.md).[^cite_note-star-425] After moving with Netanyahu to Israel, Cates sued for divorce in 1988.[^cite_note-star-425]

His third wife, [Sara Ben-Artzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_Netanyahu), was working as a [flight attendant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_attendant) on an [El Al](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Al) flight from New York to Israel when they met.[^cite_note-bio-71] She was in the process of completing a master's degree in psychology.[^cite_note-426] The couple married in 1991. They have two sons: [Yair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yair_Netanyahu) (born 26 July 1991), a former soldier in the [IDF Spokesperson's Unit](5257972.md),[^cite_note-427] and Avner (born 10 October 1994), a national Bible champion, winner of the National Bible Quiz for Youth in [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md), and former soldier in the IDF [Combat Intelligence Collection Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_Intelligence_Collection_Corps).[^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429]

In 1993, Netanyahu confessed on live television to having an affair with Ruth Bar, his public relations adviser. He stated that a political rival had planted a [secret video camera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hidden_camera) that recorded him in a sexually compromising position with Bar and that he had been [threatened with the release of the tape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sextortion) to the press unless he withdrew from the Likud leadership race. Netanyahu and Sara repaired their marriage, and he was subsequently elected to the leadership of Likud.[^cite_note-430] In 1996, the media reported that he had a 20-year friendship with Katherine Price-Mondadori, an Italian-American woman.[^cite_note-431]

### Health

Netanyahu has been suffering from [right bundle branch block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_bundle_branch_block) (RBBB) since around 2003.[^cite_note-432] In the first half of 2008, doctors removed a small [colon polyp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colon_polyp) that proved to be benign.[^cite_note-433] On 22 July 2023, a pacemaker was implanted in his body.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435] A hernia was discovered on him in March 2024.[^cite_note-436] In December 2024, his prostate was removed following a urinary tract infection caused by an enlargement.[^cite_note-437]

His personal physician and close friend is the Romanian-born pediatrist [Herman Berkovits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herman_Berkovits).[^cite_note-438]

Netanyahu was falsely reported as dead in March 2026, and subsequently posted a video mocking the claims, which was verified by [Reuters](18998750.md).[^cite_note-439] According to [i24NEWS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I24NEWS_\(Israeli_TV_channel\)), the rumor was started with a false report by Iranian [Tasnim News Agency](42800472.md).[^cite_note-440]

In April 2026, Netanyahu announced that he had been treated for early-stage [prostate cancer](88078.md).[^cite_note-441] The [Israeli Prime Minister's Office](27756022.md) disclosed that he had undergone surgery in December 2024, which was not revealed then.[^cite_note-442]

In August 2026, Netanyahu claimed that the Iranian regime attempted to murder one of his children.[^cite_note-443]
